# Assign an Activity — the runbook

The repeatable path for putting an LSAT U Activity into a student's hands.
Written 2026-09-07 after walking it live on lsatprep.study.

Joshua says **"assign &lt;activity&gt; to &lt;student&gt;"** and this is what happens,
end to end, with no further questions in the normal case.

Companion: [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]] — which platform account is which
student. Read it before ticking a name.

## Standing authorization

Joshua granted this on 2026-09-07: assigning an Activity to a **named student**
is a **go** — do it, then report. Do not stop to ask permission first.

This is a deliberate exception to the global "draft, never send" rule, which
still holds for email, posts, and messages. It exists because assignment is the
core loop of the tutoring business and confirming each one made it useless.

**Still confirm first when:**

- assigning to **3 or more students at once** (each one emails a real person)
- the name is **ambiguous** — most importantly **Steven Morris vs "Stevie"**,
  two accounts that may be one person
- the student is on the unresolved list: **Thaniuska Vivas** (no account),
  **Amanda** (no account found)
- Joshua names a student who is **not in the identity map at all**

## The path

The Activity Centre lives at `/activities` — Classroom sidebar → **Study** →
**Activities**. Joshua's account is admin, so each card in **The library** carries
its own assign button.

1. Navigate to `https://lsatprep.study/activities`
2. `find` the button **"Assign &lt;Activity name&gt;"** (one per card, exact title)
3. Click it — a dialog opens, titled **"Assign: &lt;Activity name&gt;"**
4. Optional: fill **"Schedule for"** (a date input) if Joshua named a due date
5. `read_page` the dialog and locate the student's checkbox by display name
6. Click the checkbox — the footer button updates to **"Assign to 1 students"**
7. Click that button
8. Confirm the result, then report what was sent

The dialog is a portal — it renders **outside `<main>`**, so `get_page_text` will
not show it. Use `read_page` (with the dialog's `ref_id` to keep output small).

## The nine Activities

Assign by the exact card title:

| # | World | Activity | Grain |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | The Specimen Lab | Vocab Rehab | a word |
| 02 | The Signal Observatory | Spot the Relationship | a sentence |
| 03 | The Switchyard | Conditional Rephrase | a statement, translated |
| 04 | The Polarity Chamber | Negation Station | a statement, negated |
| 05 | The Argument Atelier | Find the Conclusion | an argument |
| 06 | The Debate Chamber | Rebuttal Rephrase | an argument, restated |
| 07 | The Press | Dumb it Down | a paragraph |
| 08 | The Loom | Spot the Callback | a passage |
| 09 | Out Loud | Read, Recall, Colour | a whole section, against the clock |

### Read, Recall, Colour

Assign **card 09** — the section-library door. The student then chooses any of
the 12 seeded sections, each 25 minutes:

PT1.S3 · PT21.S3 · PT101.S2 · PT102.S3 · PT103.S1 · PT104.S1 · PT105.S1 ·
PT105.S2 · PT106.S2 · PT112.S4 · PT134.S3 · PT140.S1

Every attempt is kept. Re-running a section saves a **new attempt**; it never
replaces the old one.

Only assign an individual `read-recall-colour-pt*-s*` slug when Joshua
deliberately wants her pinned to one section. The door is the default.

## What assigning actually does

`assignWork()` — `projects/lsatu/src/lib/assignWork.ts`:

1. **Snapshots** the Activity into `activity_assignments`. The snapshot is
   immutable once issued, so later content edits do not reach an assignment
   already sent.
2. **Writes a `classroom_items` row** — this is what makes it reachable at all.
3. **Notifies on four channels**: inbox message, bell, email, classroom item.

Notification failure never fails the assignment. A student can therefore hold an
assignment she was never told about — worth checking if she says she got nothing.

## Verifying it landed

After assigning, the sheet closes and a toast confirms. To verify properly:
open `/teacher` → the student's record, or have her Classroom show the item
under **Assigned work**.

If she says she cannot see it: check her **Activities access flag** first.
Flags resolve in `projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. An assignment
lands regardless of flags, but the surface that displays it may be gated.

## Defects to mention when assigning Read, Recall, Colour

As of 2026-09-07 these are live and unfixed. Tell her, or fix them first:

- The door's heading reads **"Recall Rephrase Colour"** — a name that is not the
  product's — directly under a pill reading "Read, Recall, Colour".
  Hardcoded at `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:281`; the aria-label at :314 repeats it;
  `SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx:54` asserts the wrong string, which is why the
  2,643-test suite stays green.
- Step 1's copy in production is the old *"Intake only…"* wording. The rewrite
  and its migration `20260906220000_simplify_recall_read_step.sql` are
  uncommitted and have never run.
- Attempt dates can render out of order — PT134.S3 shows Attempt 1 dated after
  Attempt 3. This is the `graded_at` vs `created_at` bug named in `PLAN.md`
  item 5.

## Verified working (2026-09-07)

- All 9 Activities seeded and listed
- All 12 RRC sections seeded and runnable
- `attempt_no` **is** applied in production — multi-attempt history renders
- 33 accounts in the picker; 20 map to real students

/**
 * Headless assign: put an Activity, a guided drill, or a plain message into a
 * student's LSAT U account from the command line.
 *
 * Run with vite-node so `@/` resolves and this calls the app's OWN assignWork()
 * rather than a second implementation of it. That matters: assigning touches
 * seven tables (assignment snapshot, classroom item, conversation, message,
 * notification, plus the email edge function) and rolls back on failure. A
 * reimplementation would drift from the UI the first time the app changed.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --drill "main-conclusion-1"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --message "Start with PT105.S2."
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "..." --dry-run
 *
 * Credentials are read from ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored), never from this repo's
 * .env, which IS tracked in git. Same convention as scripts/db-pull.mjs.
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { assignWork } from "@/lib/assignWork";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;
}

const env = {
  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")),
  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")),
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- arguments

function arg(name: string): string | undefined {
  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);
  return i >= 0 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;
}
const has = (name: string) => process.argv.includes(`--${name}`);

const studentQuery = arg("student");
const activityQuery = arg("activity");
const drillQuery = arg("drill");
const messageText = arg("message");
const scheduleFor = arg("schedule");
const dryRun = has("dry-run");

if (!studentQuery || (!activityQuery && !drillQuery && !messageText)) {
  console.error(
    "usage: vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student <name|email> \\\n" +
      "         (--activity <title> | --drill <slug|title> | --message <text>)\\\n" +
      "         [--schedule YYYY-MM-DD] [--dry-run]",
  );
  process.exit(2);
}
if (scheduleFor && !/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(scheduleFor)) {
  console.error(`--schedule must be YYYY-MM-DD, got "${scheduleFor}"`);
  process.exit(2);
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------- signing in

/**
 * Sign in as Joshua. The service_role key would bypass RLS entirely, so it is
 * only honoured when explicitly present; the default is a normal admin login,
 * which exercises the same permission path the UI does. A script that quietly
 * ran as service_role would hide an RLS regression instead of hitting it.
 */

# Assign work on LSAT U

Put an Activity, a guided drill, or a message into a student's LSAT U account
from the command line. No browser, no clicking.

## Authorization

Joshua pre-authorized this on 2026-09-07: **a single named student is a go.**
Run it, then report. Do not stop to ask permission first.

Confirm before running when:

- assigning to **3 or more students at once** — each one emails a real person
- the student name is **ambiguous** (the script refuses and lists the matches)
- the student is **Thaniuska Vivas** or **Amanda** — no platform account found
- Joshua names someone absent from [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]

This is a deliberate, narrow exception to his global "draft, never send" rule.
That rule still holds for email, posts, and anything outside LSAT U.

## The command

Run from `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu`:

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"
```

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Lydia" --drill "main-conclusion-1"
```

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Dakota" --message "Start with PT105.S2 before Thursday."
```

Flags: `--schedule YYYY-MM-DD` unlocks the work that morning. `--dry-run`
resolves the student and the work and writes nothing — use it when the name is
at all uncertain.

To pace a student over several days, run the command once per day with a
`--schedule` on each. Each run is its own assignment, its own row, and its own
email, so she is told about each day rather than handed a block.

Every assignment lands on the home calendar strip and the cell opens that
assignment — scheduled work on its unlock day, unscheduled work on the day you
assigned it. Nothing needs to be written by hand to put work on the calendar;
if an assignment is missing from the band, that is a bug to fix in
`useClassroomHome`, not something to patch into `classroom_items`.

Always `--dry-run` first for a student you have not assigned to before in this
session. It costs one second and it is the only cheap way to catch a wrong
account.

## Why a script and not a direct DB write

`scripts/assign.ts` calls the app's **own** `assignWork()` from
`src/lib/assignWork.ts`. Assigning touches seven tables — the assignment
snapshot, `classroom_items`, the conversation and its participants, `messages`,
`notifications` — plus the email edge function, and it rolls the whole thing
back if the classroom item or notify throws. There is no RPC and no edge
function for it; it is client-side code.

So never hand-write these rows. A second implementation drifts from the UI the
first time the app changes, and produces assignments subtly unlike the ones
Joshua makes by clicking.

## Credentials

Read from `~/ai-os/.env`, which is **not** a git repo:

- `LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL`, `LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` — already set

Never put a credential in `projects/lsatu/.env`. That file **is tracked in git**
and would be committed. The script signs in as Joshua and works under normal
admin RLS, the same permission path the UI uses.

`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is deliberately **not** used even when present: it
bypasses RLS on every write, so a script running as service_role would hide an
RLS regression instead of hitting it. The script warns and falls back.

## Expected output

```
Assign "Read, Recall, Colour" → Celia <celiacutler@gmail.com>
Assigned. id=e83e2193-…
  inbox ok · bell ok · email ok
```

A notify channel reading `FAILED` is **not** an assignment failure — the work is
in her Classroom and only a message went missing. Report it, do not re-run.

## Failure modes

**`duplicate key … activity_assignments_active_unique`** — she already holds an
active assignment for this Activity. Not a bug; the DB refusing a double-assign.
Check what she has before re-trying (below).

**`"X" is ambiguous`** — the script lists every matching account and stops.
Pick the full name or the email. Never guess: a misdirected assignment is
silent, because she simply never sees it.

**Sign-in failed** — the credentials in `~/ai-os/.env` are wrong or the account
gained MFA.

## Checking what a student already has

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "..." --dry-run
```
resolves her identity. For her actual assignments, query
`activity_assignments` filtered on `student_id`, ordered by `created_at desc`.

## Read, Recall, Colour specifically

Assign the title **"Read, Recall, Colour"** — the section-library door. She then
picks any of 12 seeded sections (PT1.S3, PT21.S3, PT101.S2, PT102.S3, PT103.S1,
PT104.S1, PT105.S1, PT105.S2, PT106.S2, PT112.S4, PT134.S3, PT140.S1), 25 min
each. Every attempt is kept; re-running saves a new attempt.

The script prefers the exact title, so it resolves to the door rather than one
of the 12 sections. Pass a `read-recall-colour-pt*-s*` slug only to pin her to
one section deliberately.

import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from "react";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import { useClassId } from "@/hooks/useClassId";

/**
 * Admin-scoped CRUD for homework_sets.
 * RLS enforces created_by = auth.uid() and has_role(..., 'admin').
 */

export type InstructionLayout = 'two_column' | 'single_column';

export interface HomeworkSet {
  id: string;
  title: string;
  description: string | null;
  question_qids: string[];
  created_by: string;
  class_id: string;
  created_at: string;
  updated_at: string;
  /** Guided-set extension fields (nullable on legacy rows). */
  is_guided_set?: boolean;
  instructions_markdown?: string | null;
  instruction_layout?: InstructionLayout | null;
  guided_set_type?: string | null;
  question_type?: string | null;
  reasoning_type?: string | null;
  question_feature?: string | null;
  estimated_minutes?: number | null;
  source_pts?: string[] | null;
  difficulty_min?: number | null;
  difficulty_max?: number | null;
  start_button_label?: string | null;
}

export interface GuidedSetFields {
  is_guided_set?: boolean;
  instructions_markdown?: string | null;
  instruction_layout?: InstructionLayout | null;
  guided_set_type?: string | null;
  question_type?: string | null;
  reasoning_type?: string | null;
  question_feature?: string | null;
  estimated_minutes?: number | null;
  source_pts?: string[] | null;
  difficulty_min?: number | null;
  difficulty_max?: number | null;
  start_button_label?: string | null;
}

export interface CreateHomeworkSetInput extends GuidedSetFields {
  title: string;
  description?: string | null;
  question_qids: string[];
}

export interface UpdateHomeworkSetInput extends GuidedSetFields {
  title?: string;
  description?: string | null;
  question_qids?: string[];
}

export interface MutationResult<T> {
  data: T | null;
  error: string | null;
}

export interface UseHomeworkSetsResult {
  sets: HomeworkSet[];
  loading: boolean;
  error: string | null;
  classIdReady: boolean;
  refresh: () => Promise<void>;
  create: (input: CreateHomeworkSetInput) => Promise<MutationResult<HomeworkSet>>;
  update: (id: string, input: UpdateHomeworkSetInput) => Promise<MutationResult<HomeworkSet>>;
  remove: (id: string) => Promise<{ ok: boolean; error: string | null }>;
}

---
import { defineTool } from "@lovable.dev/mcp-js";
import { z } from "zod";
import { adminRpc, authenticatedSupabase, toolError } from "../admin-client";
import { sendNotifyEmail } from "../notify-email";

export default defineTool({
  name: "assign_homework",
  title: "Assign homework set to a student (admin)",
  description:
    "Admin only. Assign an existing homework set to a student and notify them (bell + inbox + email). Creates a homework_assignments row and a Classroom feed item. Get the set id from list_homework_sets and the student id from list_students.",
  inputSchema: {
    set_id: z.string().uuid().describe("homework_sets.id to assign."),
    student_id: z.string().uuid().describe("Target student's id (profiles.class_id / auth user id)."),
  },
  annotations: { readOnlyHint: false, destructiveHint: false, openWorldHint: false },
  handler: async ({ set_id, student_id }, ctx) => {
    try {
      const supabase = authenticatedSupabase(ctx);

      // Redirect through the notifying RPC (bell + inbox) instead of the old
      // silent direct inserts. One assignment per (set, student), so key the
      // idempotency on that pair — a retry returns the same result.
      const idempotencyKey = `assign_homework_set-${set_id}-${student_id}`;

      const { data, error } = await adminRpc(supabase, "mcp_assign_homework_set", {
        p_set_id: set_id,
        p_student_id: student_id,
        p_idempotency_key: idempotencyKey,
        p_notify: true,
      });
      if (error) throw error;

      // Email only on a fresh assignment we actually notified for — not on an
      // already-assigned no-op or a reused idempotent result.
      let out = data as Record<string, unknown>;
      if (
        out?.ok === true &&
        out?.reused !== true &&
        out?.already_assigned !== true &&
        typeof out?.route === "string"
      ) {
        const email = await sendNotifyEmail(supabase, {
          studentId: student_id,
          title: typeof out.title === "string" ? out.title : "New homework",
          route: out.route as string,
          assignmentType: "homework",
        });
        out = { ...out, email_ok: email.ok, ...(email.error ? { email_error: email.error } : {}) };
      }

      return {
        content: [{ type: "text", text: JSON.stringify(out, null, 2) }],
        structuredContent: out,
      };
    } catch (error) {
      return toolError(error);
    }
  },
});

async function signIn(): Promise<string> {
  if (env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY) {
    console.warn(
      "! SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is set but this script signs in as the admin user.\n" +
        "  Using it would require injecting a second client into assignWork, which would\n" +
        "  bypass RLS for every write. Falling back to the admin login.",
    );
  }
  const email = env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL;
  const password = [REDACTED];
  if (!email || !password) {
    console.error(
      "assign: LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL / LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD are not set.\n" +
        "  Add them to ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored). This repo's .env is TRACKED in git,\n" +
        "  so a credential placed there would be committed.",
    );
    process.exit(2);
  }
  const { data, error } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });
  if (error || !data.user) {
    console.error(`assign: sign-in failed — ${error?.message ?? "no user returned"}`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return data.user.id;
}

// -------------------------------------------------------------- resolution

interface StudentRow {
  class_id: string;
  display_name: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  [k: string]: unknown;
}

/**
 * Students come from the admin-manage-users edge function, which cross-joins
 * profiles with auth.users for the email. `class_id` is the canonical student
 * identifier everywhere in this codebase.
 */
async function listStudents(): Promise<StudentRow[]> {
  const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke("admin-manage-users", {
    method: "GET",
  });
  if (error) throw new Error(`admin-manage-users failed: ${error.message}`);
  const rows = (data ?? []) as StudentRow[];
  if (!Array.isArray(rows)) throw new Error("admin-manage-users returned no user list");
  return rows;
}

/**
 * Resolve a name or email to exactly one student. Ambiguity is fatal rather
 * than resolved by picking the first: sending a student's homework to the wrong
 * account is silent — she simply never sees it, and he never learns why.
 */
function resolveStudent(rows: StudentRow[], query: string): StudentRow {
  const q = query.trim().toLowerCase();
  const exact = rows.filter(
    (r) => r.email?.toLowerCase() === q || r.display_name?.toLowerCase() === q,
  );
  const pool = exact.length ? exact : rows.filter(
    (r) =>
      r.display_name?.toLowerCase().includes(q) || r.email?.toLowerCase().includes(q),
  );

  if (pool.length === 0) {
    console.error(`assign: no student matches "${query}".`);
    console.error("Known:", rows.map((r) => r.display_name ?? r.email).join(" · "));
    process.exit(1);
  }
  if (pool.length > 1) {
    console.error(`assign: "${query}" is ambiguous — ${pool.length} accounts match:`);
    for (const r of pool) console.error(`  - ${r.display_name ?? "(no name)"}  <${r.email ?? "?"}>`);
    console.error("Narrow it with the full name or the email address.");
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return pool[0];
}

async function resolveActivity(query: string) {
  const q = query.trim();
  const bySlug = await supabase.from("activities").select("id,title,slug,mode").eq("slug", q).maybeSingle();
  if (bySlug.data) return bySlug.data;

  const { data, error } = await supabase
    .from("activities")
    .select("id,title,slug,mode")
    .ilike("title", `%${q}%`);
  if (error) throw error;
  const rows = data ?? [];

  // "Read, Recall, Colour" matches the door and all 12 sections. Prefer the
  // exact title, which is the door — the surface that lets her pick a section.
  const exact = rows.filter((r) => r.title.toLowerCase() === q.toLowerCase());
  const pool = exact.length ? exact : rows;

  if (pool.length === 0) {
    console.error(`assign: no Activity matches "${query}".`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  if (pool.length > 1) {
    console.error(`assign: "${query}" matches ${pool.length} Activities:`);
    for (const r of pool) console.error(`  - ${r.title}  (${r.slug})`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return pool[0];
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- messaging

/** Mirrors findOrCreateThread in src/lib/classroomNotify.ts, which is private. */
async function findOrCreateThread(adminUserId: string, studentId: string): Promise<string> {
  const { data: mine } = await supabase
    .from("conversation_participants")
    .select("conversation_id")
    .eq("user_id", adminUserId);
  const { data: theirs } = await supabase
    .from("conversation_participants")
    .select("conversation_id")
    .eq("user_id", studentId);

  const ids = new Set((mine ?? []).map((r) => r.conversation_id));
  const shared = (theirs ?? []).find((r) => ids.has(r.conversation_id));
  if (shared) return shared.conversation_id as string;

  const { data: conv, error } = await supabase
    .from("conversations")
    .insert({})
    .select("id")
    .single();
  if (error || !conv) throw error ?? new Error("could not create conversation");

  const { error: pErr } = await supabase.from("conversation_participants").insert([
    { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: adminUserId },
    { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: studentId },
  ]);
  if (pErr) throw pErr;
  return conv.id as string;
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------- main

// ------------------------------------------------------------ access

/**
 * The flag that has to be on for a student to open what she was just assigned.
 *
 * Every route into this work sits behind a `ProtectedRoute`, and Classroom's
 * own feed drops rows a student cannot open, so a missing flag does not read as
 * a locked door: the work is simply invisible, and the link Joshua sends says
 * "Not open yet".
 *
 * That happened. Ivory Johnson was assigned three Read, Recall, Colour sections
 * on 2026-09-08 with `has_activity_access` off. He mailed Joshua on the Monday
 * to say the portal was empty, tried the link on the Tuesday, was told it was
 * not in his plan, and lost a day of study to a boolean. Nothing in the assign
 * path had any opinion about whether he could reach what it was writing.
 *
 * Assigning work he cannot open is never what Joshua means, so this grants the
 * one flag the assigned work needs. It never grants anything else and it never
 * revokes: the rest of the feature set stays exactly where he set it.
 */
const ACCESS_FLAG = {
  activity: "has_activity_access",
  guided_drill: "has_drill_access",
} as const;

async function ensureCanOpen(classId: string, kind: keyof typeof ACCESS_FLAG, who: string) {
  const flag = ACCESS_FLAG[kind];
  const { data, error } = await supabase
    .from("profiles")
    .select(flag)
    .eq("class_id", classId)
    .single();
  if (error) {
    console.warn(`! Could not check ${flag} for ${who}: ${error.message}`);
    console.warn("  The work is assigned. Confirm they can open it before telling them it is there.");
    return;
  }
  if ((data as Record<string, boolean>)?.[flag]) return;

  const { error: upErr } = await supabase
    .from("profiles")
    .update({ [flag]: true })
    .eq("class_id", classId);
  if (upErr) {
    console.error(`! ${who} does not have ${flag} and it could not be turned on: ${upErr.message}`);
    console.error("  They will see \"Not open yet\". Turn it on in Admin before telling them.");
    return;
  }
  console.log(`  ${flag} was off for ${who}. Turned it on, or they could not have opened this.`);
}

async function main() {
  const teacherId = await signIn();
  const student = resolveStudent(await listStudents(), studentQuery!);
  const who = `${student.display_name ?? "(no name)"} <${student.email ?? "?"}>`;

  if (messageText) {
    console.log(`Message → ${who}\n  "${messageText}"`);
    if (dryRun) return console.log("\n(dry run — nothing sent)");
    const conversationId = await findOrCreateThread(teacherId, student.class_id);
    const { error } = await supabase
      .from("messages")
      .insert({ conversation_id: conversationId, sender_id: teacherId, body: messageText });
    if (error) throw error;
    return console.log("Sent.");
  }

  const item = activityQuery
    ? { kind: "activity" as const, activityId: (await resolveActivity(activityQuery)).id }
    : { kind: "guided_drill" as const, drillId: (await resolveDrill(drillQuery!)).id };

  const label = activityQuery
    ? (await resolveActivity(activityQuery)).title
    : (await resolveDrill(drillQuery!)).title;

  console.log(`Assign "${label}" → ${who}${scheduleFor ? `  (unlocks ${scheduleFor})` : ""}`);
  if (dryRun) return console.log("\n(dry run — nothing written)");

  const { ok, failed } = await assignWork({
    teacherId,
    studentIds: [student.class_id],
    item,
    scheduledForLocalDate: scheduleFor,
  });

export function useHomeworkSets(): UseHomeworkSetsResult {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const { classId, loading: classIdLoading } = useClassId();
  const [sets, setSets] = useState<HomeworkSet[]>([]);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);

  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {
    // Returning while `loading` is still true left the list stuck on its
    // skeleton forever for anyone signed out — a spinner that never resolves
    // reads as a broken page, not as "nothing to show."
    if (!user) {
      setLoading(false);
      return;
    }
    setLoading(true);
    const { data, error: err } = await supabase
      .from("homework_sets")
      .select("*")
      .eq("created_by", user.id)
      .order("created_at", { ascending: false });
    if (err) {
      console.error("[homework:sets:list] failed", { userId: user.id, error: err });
      setError(err.message);
      setSets([]);
    } else {
      setSets((data || []) as HomeworkSet[]);
      setError(null);
    }
    setLoading(false);
  }, [user]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (user) void refresh();
  }, [user, refresh]);

  const create = useCallback(
    async (input: CreateHomeworkSetInput): Promise<MutationResult<HomeworkSet>> => {
      if (!user) return { data: null, error: "Not signed in." };
      if (classIdLoading) return { data: null, error: "Still loading your class. Try again in a moment." };
      const effectiveClassId = classId || user.id;
      const guidedFields: Record<string, unknown> = {};
      const guidedKeys: (keyof GuidedSetFields)[] = [
        'is_guided_set', 'instructions_markdown', 'instruction_layout',
        'guided_set_type', 'question_type', 'reasoning_type', 'question_feature',
        'estimated_minutes', 'source_pts', 'difficulty_min', 'difficulty_max',
        'start_button_label',
      ];
      for (const k of guidedKeys) {
        const v = input[k];
        if (v !== undefined) guidedFields[k] = v;
      }
      // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
      const { data, error: err } = await supabase
        .from("homework_sets")
        .insert({
          title: input.title,
          description: input.description ?? null,
          question_qids: input.question_qids,
          created_by: user.id,
          class_id: effectiveClassId,

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS homework_assignments_student_status_idx
  ON public.homework_assignments (student_id, status, created_at DESC);

-- At most one active assignment per (student, set). A completed assignment
-- does not block re-issuing the same set.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS homework_assignments_active_unique
  ON public.homework_assignments (student_id, set_id)
  WHERE status <> 'completed' AND set_id IS NOT NULL;

---
Directions
A
a
Section Time Remaining: 34:59

When primatologist Akira Suzuki began studying snow monkeys in the 1950s, he found that they often roamed out of the mountains to feed in apple orchards. After a decade of observing this behavior, Suzuki began to feed the monkeys in their mountain habitat by providing them with soybeans to eat. The monkeys no longer raided the orchards. When Suzuki began his work, 23 snow monkeys lived in the region he studied. The population today is 270 snow monkeys and is expected to continue growing.

1.

Which one of the following claims is most strongly supported by the information above?

A
Snow monkeys do not feed outside of their mountain habitat when food is readily available within it.
B
For snow monkeys, soybeans provide more complete nutrition than

[truncated to 800 chars]

# Grill Me

Relentlessly interview the user about every aspect of the topic until you reach shared understanding. Walk down each branch of the decision tree, resolving dependencies one by one. The real goal is to **extract what's in their head into a durable, organized markdown file** so nothing is lost as context fills up.

## The capture file is the whole point

Long interviews fill up context. If you hold answers only in your head, you will eventually misremember, conflate, or drop something. So you **checkpoint to disk after every single answer**. The file, not your context, is the source of truth. Never make the user ask you to save progress.

## Existing context and scope

Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`, but follow an established project's declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.

## Setup (do this BEFORE the first question)

1. **Create the capture file** at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create the `brainstorms/` folder if it doesn't exist). Every brainstorm capture lives here. One predictable home, regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. If a session later produces a polished deliverable (a plan, a map, a spec), that artifact can move into the relevant `projects/` folder, but the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
   - Get today's actual date using the available clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
   - Never overwrite an existing capture. For a new session with the same date/topic, add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user requests or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.
   - If no topic is supplied or inferable, create an `untitled-discovery` capture with goal pending, then ask what to explore. Record the chosen topic and goal in that same file.
2. **Create the file immediately** with a header: title, date, the goal of the session, and an empty "Open flags" section.
3. **Tell the user where you're saving**, in one line. Then ask Q1.

## The checkpoint rule (non-negotiable)

After EVERY user answer, BEFORE you ask the next question:
- Append a structured entry to the capture file: the question topic, the key facts and decisions from their answer (in their words where the wording matters), and any flags (things they couldn't answer plus who should).
- Update the running summary when a later answer changes it. Preserve the original Q&A entry, mark it superseded, and link to the correcting answer so the reasoning remains traceable.
- Read back the saved entry before continuing. If saving fails, explain the failure and keep the answer visible in chat; do not claim it was saved or continue collecting answers without a working checkpoint.
- Only then ask the next question.

Never batch multiple answers into one write. Checkpoint one answer at a time. The point is that if context is lost at any moment, the file already holds everything said so far.

## Interview method

- Ask **one question at a time**. For decisions, provide a suggested answer grounded in context and label it as a suggestion. For personal or business facts, ask neutrally; do not lead the user with invented facts or save your inference as their answer.
- Keep user-confirmed facts, tentative ideas, assistant suggestions, and unresolved questions distinguishable in the capture.
- Resolve dependencies in order: settle the upstream decision before the ones that depend on it.
- If a question can be answered by **exploring the codebase or reading a file/doc**, do that instead of asking. If the user hands you a doc (e.g. a Google Doc), read it and only surface what's net-new.
- When the user **can't answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Don't stall.
- Keep going until the user says you're done, needs a pause, or you've covered the useful branches. Respect an explicit stop immediately; do not add a new question. Offer a completeness backstop near the end ("anything we haven't touched?").

## Capture file structure

```
# {Topic}: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes
Date: {date} · Goal: {one line}
Status: in progress / paused / complete
Context sources: {relevant existing pages, if any}

## Summary / key decisions
(running synthesis, updated as you go)

## Q&A log
### Q1 - {topic}
- Asked: {question}
- Captured: {user-confirmed facts, decisions, in their words where it matters}
- Tentative / suggested: {unconfirmed ideas, clearly labeled}
- Flags: {open item -> owner}
...

## Open flags (pending input)
- {item} -> {who can answer}
```

## At the end or on pause
- Read the capture for contradictions or gaps. Mark unresolved conflicts explicitly; do not choose a business fact on the user's behalf. Update the session status and resume point.
- For sessions explicitly about building or updating AI OS context, merge user-confirmed durable facts and preferences into the appropriate existing context page, with a dated link back to the capture. Preserve unrelated content. Record confirmed meaningful decisions in the existing decision log without duplicating entries. Unknowns, brainstorm ideas, and assistant suggestions stay labeled in the capture until confirmed.
- For a plan/design interview without a request to update canonical context, keep the raw capture as the output. Suggest `/link` for routing a useful result, or `/level-up` for turning a selected opportunity into one improvement. Do not run either automatically.
- Ensure the operating manual or an existing index routes to `brainstorms/` when capture routing is absent, using the project's conventions and preserving shared manual parity. Individual captures do not each need a root-manual entry. Raw captures are dated interview evidence, not automatically current business truth.
- Give a short recap with a clickable capture path, context pages actually updated, remaining flags, and the next step or resume point.

## Boundaries and verification

- Local interview capture and requested context updates are the scope. No global-memory edits, publication, messages, or external-system changes. Do not request or save credentials; use references or redactions for secrets.
- The starter kit ignores `brainstorms/` in Git by default. This does not remove captures already tracked in an existing repository.
- Validate a fresh session, same-day name collision, resume, a correcting answer, a stop request, and a failed write. Verify that only confirmed facts reach canonical context and that earlier answers remain traceable.

function PracticeTestSelector({
  tests,
  onBegin,
}: {
  tests: PracticeTestBlueprint[];
  onBegin: (
    blueprint: PracticeTestBlueprint,
    pace: PracticeTestPaceId,
    sectionSeconds: number | null,
  ) => void;
}) {
  const [pt, setPt] = React.useState<number | null>(null);
  const [pace, setPace] = React.useState<PracticeTestPaceId>("standard");
  const { settings } = useUserSettings();

  /**
   * The student's own standard clock, the same reading the section door takes
   * (SectionSelector's baseMinutes). Every pace here is a percentage of this,
   * so "Standard" is their normal section rather than a flat 35 minutes. This
   * door ignored the setting entirely before, which meant the same two words
   * named two different clocks in one room.
          </SelectContent>
        </Select>
        {/* The section door has carried this since its timing control was
            built. Without it this door offered accommodations while saying
            nothing about the standing setting those percentages are taken
            from, on the longer of the two commitments. */}
        <p className="testing-note">
          <AccommodationNote baseMinutes={baseMinutes} />
        </p>
      </div>

      {chosen && (
        <>
          <div className="testing-shape">
            {chosen.legs.map((leg, i) => (
              <div className="testing-shape-row" key={`${leg.kind}-${leg.section}`}>
                <b>Section {i + 1}</b>
                <span>{legLabel(leg as never)}</span>
                <em>
                  {leg.questionCount} questions
                  {clock ? ` · ${clock}` : ""}
                </em>
              </div>
            ))}
          </div>
          <p className="testing-note">
            {chosen.questionCount} scored questions, with a ten-minute break
            after the second section.
          </p>
          {/* The clock is read from the chosen pace rather than printed as
              35:00. It was hard-coded, which stopped being true the moment this
              door started honouring the student's own timing mode: an
              accommodated student saw 52:30 on the control and "the real 35:00"
              in the paragraph under it. The clause drops out entirely when
              there is no clock, rather than asserting a number for a sitting
              that is not being timed. */}
          {!chosen.rcFullLength && (
            <p className="testing-note">
              The Reading Comprehension section on this PrepTest runs a few
              questions short, because only questions with a published
              explanation can be drilled. All four passages are there
              {clock ? ` and the clock is the real ${clock}` : ""}, so the
              reading load is unchanged. Your score is simply out of{" "}
              {chosen.questionCount} rather than the high seventies.
            </p>
          )}
        </>

          user never meets it and never learns why the door will not open;

          unavailable, and a press on it goes to the field that is missing.
          The line beside it names the missing field for everyone, and is a
          live region so the change is heard when the PrepTest is chosen. */}
      <div className="testing-actions">
        <Button
          aria-disabled={!chosen}
          aria-describedby={chosen ? undefined : beginHintId}
          className="aria-disabled:cursor-not-allowed aria-disabled:opacity-50"
          onClick={() => {
            if (chosen) {
              onBegin(chosen, pace, sectionSeconds);
              return;
            }
            document.getElementById("testing-pt")?.focus();
          }}
        >
          Begin practice test
        </Button>
        <p className="testing-note testing-actions-hint" id={beginHintId} role="status">
          {chosen ? null : "Choose a PrepTest above to begin."}
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>

## assistant

Saved, with a finding that changes the shape of this: the timing setting the Testing Centre reads (`defaultTimingMode`: 35 / 52.5 / 70 / unlimited) lives **only in the student's browser localStorage**. It is not in the database. So today you can't set it for a student, it doesn't follow her between laptop and phone, and the assign script can't read or check it.

**Q5 — Where should a student's accommodation live in the app?**

- **A. A column on her profile, set by you (suggested).** `profiles.timing_multiplier` (or similar) with 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0. Admin can set it; the assign script prints it ("Avneet: 1.5x, 52:30") and refuses or warns if the student file says otherwise; the card and the section clock read it. The student's browser setting becomes a per-session override at most.
- **B. Per assignment.** The accommodation is passed at assign time (`--time 1.5x`) and stored on the homework assignment. Simple, but you'd have to remember it on every assign and it wouldn't reach the Testing Centre.
- **C. Both:** profile is the default, assignment can override for one sitting.

      // reappearing on Home.

        route,
        selfCompleting:
          (item.metadata_json as { kind?: string } | null)?.kind === 'timed_section',
        briefing: readSectionBriefing(item.metadata_json) ?? undefined,
      });
    }

    // Open work first, then completed; newest within each bucket.
    return [...byIdentity.values()].sort((x, y) => {
      const s = (x.status === 'completed' ? 1 : 0) - (y.status === 'completed' ? 1 : 0);
      if (s !== 0) return s;
      return new Date(y.createdAt).getTime() - new Date(x.createdAt).getTime();
    });
  }
}

/**
 * A timed section carries everything its briefing needs on the classroom item
 * itself. Reading it here means Home can raise the instructions in place rather
 * than sending the student to a page whose only job was to hold them.
 *
 * The authored `content_html` on the item stays where it is: it is what the
 * emailed copy and the direct link render. This is the same assignment said in
 * the fewest words that still bind, for the moment just before the clock.
 */
export interface SectionBriefing {
  /** Minutes on the clock. Accommodated timings land here exactly. */
  minutes: number;
  /** "Logical Reasoning", "Reading Comprehension", "Logic Games". */
  sectionName: string;
  /** Where Enter goes. Already carries the sealed flag and the return path. */
  href: string;
  /** Sealed runs withhold every answer and explanation for the whole sitting. */
  sealed: boolean;
}

const SECTION_NAMES: Record<string, string> = {
  lr: "Logical Reasoning",
  rc: "Reading Comprehension",
  lg: "Logic Games",
};

/**
 * Null for anything that is not a timed section, which is most work. Callers
 * treat null as "this opens the ordinary way", so a malformed row degrades to
 * the old navigation rather than to a briefing with holes in it.
 */
export function readSectionBriefing(metadata: unknown): SectionBriefing | null {
  if (!metadata || typeof metadata !== "object") return null;
  const meta = metadata as Record<string, unknown>;
  if (meta.kind !== "timed_section") return null;

  const minutes = typeof meta.minutes === "number" ? meta.minutes : null;
  const href = typeof meta.href === "string" ? meta.href : null;
  if (!minutes || minutes <= 0 || !href) return null;

  const sectionKind = typeof meta.section_kind === "string" ? meta.section_kind : "";
  return {
    minutes,
    sectionName: SECTION_NAMES[sectionKind] ?? "Timed Section",
    href,
    sealed: meta.sealed === true,
  };
}

/**
 * The last screen before the clock.
 *
 * It covers the stage rather than replacing the page: the student stays on
 * Home, the rail and the bento go quiet underneath, and nothing about the
 * session is committed until Enter. Pressing Escape or "Not now" puts Home
 * back exactly as it was, which is the point of not navigating.
 *
 * The copy is deliberately short. This is read in the thirty seconds before a
 * timed section, standing up, and every line that is not a condition of
 * sitting it well is a line that costs attention it needs.
 */
export function SectionBriefing({
  briefing,
  onStart,
  onDismiss,
}: {
  briefing: SectionBriefingData;
  onStart: () => void;
  onDismiss: () => void;
}) {
  const startRef = React.useRef<HTMLButtonElement>(null);
  React.useEffect(() => {
    startRef.current?.focus();
  }, []);
  React.useEffect(() => {
    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (e.key === "Escape") onDismiss();
    };
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
  }, [onDismiss]);
  // The page behind must not scroll while the briefing is up.
  React.useEffect(() => {
    const previous = document.body.style.overflow;
    document.body.style.overflow = "hidden";
    return () => {
      document.body.style.overflow = previous;
    };
  }, []);
  return (
    <div
      className="section-briefing"
      role="dialog"
      aria-modal="true"
      aria-labelledby="section-briefing-title"
    >
      <div className="section-briefing-inner">
        <header className="section-briefing-head">

export function primaryLabelFor(kind: HomeKind, started: boolean): string {
  const pair = PRIMARY_LABEL[kind];
  return started ? pair.resume : pair.start;
}

/**
 * The hero shows its kind as a tag, so the meta line beneath must not open by
 * saying it again. Falls back to the whole line when the kind is not the lead.
 */
export function stripKind(meta: string, kind: HomeKind): string {
  const parts = meta.split(" · ");
  if (parts.length > 1 && parts[0] === KIND_LABEL[kind]) return parts.slice(1).join(" · ");
  return meta;
}

## assistant

Found it. Here's why each button is there.

**"Open the set" vs "Open assigned work"**

They're not two ways to open the same thing. "Open the set" opens this assignment. "Open assigned work" is hard-coded on every hero card ([ClassroomHomeView.tsx:290](projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch/src/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHomeView.tsx:290)) and just switches to the Assigned-work list, the column you saw in the video. It made some sense when the hero was one of several; with one section assigned it reads as a duplicate. It should go, or move to the list heading.

**"Mark done"**

The app already has the rule you want, it just isn't being applied to what my script creates. The home feed hides Mark done for any classroom item whose metadata says `kind: "timed_section"` ([useAssignedTodoFeed.ts:28](projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch/src/hooks/useAssignedTodoFeed.ts:28)), with this comment in the code: *sitting it IS finishing it, and a "Mark done" button beside one can be pressed without doing the section at all.* Those items also close themselves when the section run finishes ([Drill.tsx:2633](projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch/src/pages/Drill.tsx:2633) → `closeOutAssignedSection`).

My `--section` path writes the assignment through `homework_sets`, which stamps the item as plain `homework`, so the feed treats it like a marked-by-hand set: Mark done shows, and finishing the section doesn't close the card.

There are actually two half-built section paths in the app:

| | `timed_section` item (older) | `homework_sets` path (what I wired) |
|---|---|---|
| Home button | opens an in-place "briefing" overlay, Enter starts the clock | "Open the set" → detail page → Start |
| Mark done | hidden | shown |
| Completes itself | yes, when the run finishes | no |
| Timed launch | yes | yes (since #113) |
| Anything writes it today | no, nothing in the codebase creates one | `scripts/assign.ts` |

So the fix is to converge them: a single-section assignment should carry the `timed_section` metadata (pt, section, kind, minutes) so it inherits the no-Mark-done rule and the self-close, and the existing briefing overlay gets replaced by the full-screen LawHub directions card from the grill. That also gives the card its minutes field, which is where the accommodated time will land.

I've added this to the capture. Want me to start building from it now (one PR: metadata + directions card + hide the second button + no Mark done), or finish the last grill question first?

export function buildAssignedRows({
  hw,
  ta,
  guided,
  activities,
  items,
}: AssignedFeedSources): AssignedTodoRow[] {
  {
    const byIdentity = new Map<string, AssignedTodoRow>();

        kind: 'homework',
        refId: a.id,
        sourceTable: 'homework_assignments',

        subtitle: `${a.question_qids?.length ?? 0} questions${a.score != null ? ` · ${Math.round(a.score)}%` : ''}`,
        status: normalizeStatus(a.status),
        createdAt: a.created_at,
        dueAt: null,
        route: `/classroom/${encodeURIComponent(a.id)}`,
      });
    }

    for (const a of ta) {
      put({
        key: `ta-${a.id}`,
        kind: 'ta',
        refId: a.id,
        sourceTable: 'ta_assignments',
        title: a.title || 'From Joshua',
        subtitle: a.pdf_url ? 'PDF attached' : 'TA assignment',
        status: a.status === 'viewed' ? 'in_progress' : normalizeStatus(a.status),
        createdAt: a.assigned_at,
        dueAt: null,
        route: `/classroom/ta/${encodeURIComponent(a.id)}`,
      });
    }

Modelled on LawHub's directions screen, because\n * the point of assigning a real section is that it feels like the real thing.\n */\n\nexport type SectionKind = 'lr' | 'rc';\n\nexport function sectionDirectionsRoute(assignmentId: string): string {\n  return `/section/${encodeURIComponent(assignmentId)}`;\n}\n\n/** LSAC's own wording, verbatim. Not to be edited for voice. */\nexport const SECTION_DIRECTIONS: Record<SectionKind, string> = {\n  lr:\n    'Each question in this section is based on the reasoning presented in a brief passage. ' +\n    'In answering the questions, you should not make assumptions that are by commonsense ' +\n    'standards implausible, superfluous, or incompatible with the passage. For some questions, ' +\n    'more than one of the choices could conceivably answer the question. However, you are to ' +\n    'choose the best answer; that is, choose the response that most accurately and completely ' +\n    'answers the question.',\n  rc:\n    'Each set of questions in this section is based on a single passage. The questions are to ' +\n    'be answered on the basis of what is stated or implied in the passage. For some questions, ' +\n    'more than one of the choices could conceivably answer the question. However, you are to ' +\n    'choose the best answer; that is, choose the response that most accurately and completely ' +\n    'answers the question.',\n};\n\n/**\n * Minutes on the clock for a timing mode, or null when there is no clock.\n * Standard is 35; 52.5 and 70 are the 1.5x and 2x accommodations.\n */\nexport function sectionMinutes(mode: TimerMode | undefined): number | null {\n  switch (mode) {\n    case '52.5':\n      return 52.5;\n    case '70':\n      return 70;\n    case 'unlimited':\n      return null;\n    default:\n      return 35;\n  }\n}\n\n/** \"35 minutes\", \"52 minutes 30 seconds\", \"70 minutes\". */\nexport function formatSectionMinutes(minutes: number): string {\n  const whole = Math.floor(minutes);\n  const seconds = Math.round((minutes - whole) * 60);\n  if (seconds === 0) return `${whole} minutes`;\n  return `${whole} minutes ${seconds} seconds`;\n}\n"}

The clock starts when GO TO QUESTIONS is pressed,\n * which is also when the assignment becomes \"in progress\", so a student who\n * opens this and walks away has lost nothing.\n *\n * It takes the whole screen on purpose. The Classroom rail and the list of\n * other work are exactly what a student should not be looking at in the\n * minute before a timed section.\n */\nexport default function SectionDirectionsPage() {\n  const { assignmentId } = useParams<{ assignmentId: string }>();\n  const navigate = useNavigate();\n  const { settings } = useUserSettings();\n  const { assignment, loading, error } = useStudentAssignment(assignmentId);\n\n  const section = React.useMemo(\n    () => (assignment ? singleSectionOf(assignment.question_qids ?? []) : null),\n    [assignment],\n  );\n\n  if (loading && !assignment) {\n    return (\n      <main className=\"section-directions\" aria-busy=\"true\">\n        <p className=\"section-directions-status\" role=\"status\">\n          Loading your section…\n        </p>\n      </main>\n    );\n  }\n\n  if (error || !assignment || !assignmentId) {\n    return (\n      <main className=\"section-directions\">\n        <div className=\"section-directions-card\">\n          <h1 className=\"section-directions-title\">Section not found</h1>\n          <p className=\"section-directions-body\">\n            This assignment could not be loaded. Open your Classroom and try again from there.\n          </p>\n        </div>\n        <button\n          type=\"button\"\n          className=\"section-directions-go\"\n          onClick={() => navigate(\"/classroom\")}\n        >\n          BACK TO CLASSROOM\n        </button>\n      </main>\n    );\n  }\n\n  // Not one section (a mixed set, or a set that was edited after assigning):\n  // the ordinary assignment page knows what to do with it.\n  if (!section) {\n    return <Navigate to={`/classroom/${encodeURIComponent(assignmentId)}`} replace />;\n  }\n\n  // Finished work is a record, not a door. Results live in History.\n  if (assignment.status === \"completed\") {\n    return <Navigate to=\"/history\" replace />;\n  }\n\n  const minutes = sectionMinutes(settings.defaultTimingMode);\n  const questionCount = assignment.question_qids.length;\n  // Only LR sections are qid-shaped today; an RC assignment will arrive with\n  // its own kind on the row rather than being inferred from a qid pattern.\n  const directions = SECTION_DIRECTIONS.lr;\n\n  const goToQuestions = () => {\n    const timerMode =\n      settings.defaultTimingMode === \"35\" ? undefined : settings.defaultTimingMode;\n    const query = launchDrill({\n      kind: \"assignment\",\n      assignmentId,\n      timerMode,\n      options: { returnTo: \"/classroom\" },\n    });\n    navigate(`/drill?${query}`);\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <main className=\"section-directions\">\n      <div className=\"section-directions-card\">\n        <h1 className=\"section-directions-title\">Section {section.section}</h1>\n        <p className=\"section-directions-line\">\n          {minutes === null\n            ? \"Untimed section\"\n            : `Total Section Time - ${formatSectionMinutes(minutes)}`}\n        </p>\n        <p className=\"section-directions-line\">\n          {questionCount} Question{questionCount === 1 ? \"\" : \"s\"}\n        </p>\n        <p className=\"section-directions-body\">\n          <strong>Directions:</strong> {directions}\n        </p>\n      </div>\n      <button\n        type=\"button\"\n        className=\"section-directions-go\"\n        onClick={goToQuestions}\n        autoFocus\n      >\n        GO TO QUESTIONS\n      </button>\n    </main>\n  );\n}\n"}

Colours are LawHub's, not\n * the app's tokens, on purpose: this screen is meant to look like the test.\n */\n.section-directions {\n  min-height: 100vh;\n  min-height: 100dvh;\n  background: #c5d5e3;\n  display: flex;\n  flex-direction: column;\n  align-items: center;\n  padding: 72px 24px 48px;\n  box-sizing: border-box;\n  font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;\n  color: #1f1f1f;\n}\n\n.section-directions-card {\n  width: 100%;\n  max-width: 1310px;\n  min-height: 430px;\n  background: #ffffff;\n  border-radius: 18px;\n  padding: 18px 84px 48px;\n  box-sizing: border-box;\n  text-align: center;\n}\n\n.section-directions-title {\n  font-size: 20px;\n  font-weight: 400;\n  margin: 0;\n  line-height: 1.5;\n}\n\n.section-directions-line {\n  font-size: 20px;\n  margin: 0;\n  line-height: 1.4;\n}\n\n.section-directions-body {\n  text-align: left;\n  font-size: 20px;\n  line-height: 1.35;\n  margin: 44px 0 0;\n}\n\n.section-directions-status {\n  font-size: 18px;\n}\n\n.section-directions-go {\n  margin-top: 32px;\n  background: #b8650b;\n  color: #ffffff;\n  border: 0;\n  border-radius: 14px;\n  padding: 18px 20px;\n  font-size: 20px;\n  font-family: inherit;\n  letter-spacing: 0.01em;\n  cursor: pointer;\n}\n\n.section-directions-go:hover,\n.section-directions-go:focus-visible {\n  background: #a3590a;\n  outline: 3px solid #1f1f1f;\n  outline-offset: 2px;\n}\n\n@media (max-width: 720px) {\n  .section-directions {\n    padding: 32px 12px 32px;\n  }\n  .section-directions-card {\n    padding: 16px 20px 32px;\n    min-height: 0;\n  }\n  .section-directions-title,\n  .section-directions-line,\n  .section-directions-body,\n  .section-directions-go {\n    font-size: 17px;\n  }\n}\n"}

## assistant

Built, tested, and pushed as [PR #121](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/121). To answer the "why": nothing was wrong with the assignment, the directions page simply did not exist yet. It does now.

**What the branch does**

- "Open the set" (and "Open" in the list, and the emailed `/classroom/…` link) for a one-section assignment now lands on `/section/:id`: white card on the blue-grey field, "Section 4", "Total Section Time - 35 minutes", "26 Questions", the official LR directions, and the orange GO TO QUESTIONS button. Full screen, no sidebar.
- The card is untimed. GO TO QUESTIONS starts the clock and the assignment.
- Mark done is gone for section assignments; finishing the section completes it (the app already wrote that, it just wasn't being applied). The duplicate "Open assigned work" button is gone from the hero.
- After the last question: score guess, then blind review opens with every question available, then results.
- First Pause in an assigned section asks "Pause the section?" with the note that the real test does not allow it; after that it pauses freely for the rest of the sitting.
- The card and the clock read the student's timing mode (35 / 52:30 / 70 / untimed).

**Verified:** typecheck clean; 9 new tests plus the existing 131 in the touched suites pass. Not yet verified with eyes, because the Browser pane needs a signed-in student and I don't enter passwords.

**To see it:** the dev server for this branch is open in the Browser pane at `localhost:8099`. Sign in there as `joshua.fisseha01@gmail.com`, tell me when you're in, and I'll click through Open the set → the card → GO TO QUESTIONS and screenshot each step. PT156.S4 is still assigned to that account, so it's ready.

**Left out of this PR, on purpose:** the profile-level accommodation column (timing mode is still a browser setting), RC section assignments, and recording pause counts for you to see. Those are the next PR once you've approved the look.

// Surfaces that own the whole screen and must stay free of floating chrome.
// The exam players are the strict case: nothing may sit over the passage, the
// choices or the footer's primary action.
const OVERLAY_FREE_PREFIXES = ["/book/", "/classroom", "/drill", "/rc"];

function GlobalOverlayMount() {
  const { pathname } = useLocation();
  if (OVERLAY_FREE_PREFIXES.some((p) => pathname === p || pathname.startsWith(p))) return null;
  return (
    <>
      <FloatingHelpButton />
    </>
  );
}

// Param/query-preserving redirects for the teacher IA rebuild (ADR 0001).
// The old surfaces still resolve; they just forward to their new home so no
// bookmarked /teacher/* URL 404s.
function RedirectBootcampToInsights() {
  const { topicId } = useParams();
  return <Navigate to={`/teacher/insights/${topicId}`} replace />;
}

  // asks — once. A section that has already been carried past its buzzer must
  // not be asked again on every tick.
  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (!timer || !session) return;
    if (session.mode !== 'full-section') return;
    if (timer.mode !== 'countdown' || timer.ms > 0) return;
    if (overtimeState !== 'none' || session.regulationMark) return;

    const answeredQids = Array.from(session.attempts.entries())
      .filter(([, a]) => !!a.selectedAnswer)
      .map(([qid]) => qid);
    setSession({ ...session, regulationMark: { answeredQids } });
    timer.pause();
    setOvertimeState('prompt');
  }, [timer, session, overtimeState]);

  // Metadata for the drawer's Details tab, drawn from the active question.
  // MUST be declared before any conditional early returns below to keep hook order stable.
  const drawerMeta = React.useMemo<DrillDetailsMeta | null>(() => {
    if (!currentQuestion) return null;
    return {
      pt: currentQuestion.pt,
      section: currentQuestion.section,
      qnum: currentQuestion.qnum,
      qtype: currentQuestion.qtype,
      reasoningType: currentQuestion.reasoningType,
      questionFeatures: currentQuestion.questionFeatures,
      difficulty: currentQuestion.difficulty,
    };
  }, [currentQuestion]);

  // ── Authored explanation for the active question ─────────────────────────
  // Declared here, above every conditional early return, so hook order stays
  // stable. One fetch feeds both surfaces: the drawer tab and the inline layer.
  const currentRevealed =
    !!currentQuestion &&
    (!!showAnswerRevealed.get(currentQuestion.qid) ||
      // Section mode's opt-in toggle: once on, an answered question reveals.
      (!revealSealed &&
        sectionRevealEnabled &&
        !!session?.attempts?.get(currentQuestion.qid)?.selectedAnswer));

  const {
    data: lrExplanation,
    isLoading: lrExplanationLoading,
    isError: lrExplanationError,
  } = useLrExplanation(
    {
      pt: currentQuestion?.pt,
      section: currentQuestion?.section,
      questionNumber: currentQuestion?.qnum,
    },
    currentRevealed,
  );

  // A style chosen on the previous question must not leak onto one that does
  // not publish it.
  React.useEffect(() => {
    setActiveStyle(null);
  }, [currentQuestion?.qid]);

/** The same carry as the older grammar wrote it, under its own param names. */
function readLegacyCarry(p: URLSearchParams, mode: DrillMode): RunCarry | undefined {
  const day = positiveNumber(p.get('bootcampDay'));
  const part = readPart(p.get('bootcampPart'));
  return buildCarry({
    mode,
    sealed: p.get('sealed') === '1',
    isGuidedDrill: p.get('guided') === '1' || !!p.get('guidedAssignmentId'),
    guidedDurationMinutes: positiveNumber(p.get('guidedDurationMinutes')),
    assignmentId: p.get('assignmentId') || undefined,
    guidedSetId: p.get('guidedSetId') || undefined,
function buildCarry(fields: Omit<RunCarry, 'mode' | 'sealed' | 'isGuidedDrill'> & {
  mode?: DrillMode;
  sealed?: boolean;
  isGuidedDrill?: boolean;
}): RunCarry | undefined {
  const { mode, sealed, isGuidedDrill, ...rest } = fields;
  const carriesSomething =
    (mode && mode !== 'type-drill') ||
    !!sealed ||
    !!isGuidedDrill ||
    Object.values(rest).some((v) => v !== undefined);
  if (!carriesSomething) return undefined;
  return {
    mode: mode ?? 'type-drill',
    sealed: !!sealed,
    isGuidedDrill: !!isGuidedDrill,
    ...rest,
  };
}

  // The buzzer. Stops the clock, records where the student had got to, and

  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (!timer || !session) return;
    if (session.mode !== 'full-section') return;
    if (timer.mode !== 'countdown' || timer.ms > 0) return;
    if (overtimeState !== 'none' || session.regulationMark) return;

    const answeredQids = Array.from(session.attempts.entries())
      .filter(([, a]) => !!a.selectedAnswer)
      .map(([qid]) => qid);
    setSession({ ...session, regulationMark: { answeredQids } });

export interface DrillExplanationContext {
  data?: LrExplanationData | null;
  isLoading?: boolean;
  isError?: boolean;
  /** Nothing is shown until the answer is revealed. */
  revealed: boolean;
  /** Shared with the inline layer, so switching style swaps both surfaces. */
  activeStyle: LrStyleKey | null;
  onStyleChange: (style: LrStyleKey) => void;
  /** Hand-off to the AI tutor when the source published no prose. */
  onAskTutor?: () => void;
}

export default function DrillExplanationTab({
  data,
  isLoading,
  isError,
  revealed,
  activeStyle,
  onStyleChange,
  onAskTutor,
}: DrillExplanationContext) {
  const styles = data?.styles ?? [];
  const current = styles.find((s) => s.style === activeStyle) ?? styles[0];

  const standardSteps = styles.find((s) => s.style === "standard")?.steps ?? [];
  const borrowsStandard =
    current != null &&
    current.style !== "standard" &&
    current.steps.length === 0 &&
    standardSteps.length > 0;
  const steps = borrowsStandard ? standardSteps : current?.steps ?? [];

  if (!revealed) {
    return (
      <Body>
        <p className="text-[13px] text-slate-400">
          The explanation unlocks once you have answered or revealed this question.
        </p>
      </Body>
    );
  }

All five are in and pushed to [PR #121](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/121). What I verified in the pane, on the josh account:

**1. Clock was stuck at 34:59.** Real bug, dev-build only. The clock started, but React's development double-mount cleared the 250 ms repaint, so the number never changed. Fixed in `useTimer`; the pane now shows it counting (27:49 → 27:44 over five seconds), and it correctly credited the minutes already spent on this sitting. Production never double-mounts, so the live site was counting down; you just couldn't see it here.

**2. Time's up.** For an assigned section the buzzer dialog now reads "Time's up." with a single button, "Go to blind review". No "Keep working". Self-run sections keep the old choice. Not exercised live (that's a 35-minute wait); covered by reading the code path, which is the same dialog that already fires.

**3. No answers during an assigned section.** Show Answer and Explanation are gone from the top strip; the drawer's Explanation tab says "Explanations open after the section is scored." Sealed is the default for every assigned section. Unsealing exists only as a launch flag (`reveal=1`) so you can ask for it at assign time later; the assign script doesn't expose it yet.

**4. Directions button.** It was showing the old paper-test text ("blacken the corresponding space on your answer sheet") in the app's dark dialog. Now it's the same digital wording as the card, white, matching the player.

**5. Pause notice.** Every section and practice test, not just assigned ones: white "Notice" card, LawHub's wording, Cancel / Ok, "Don't show me this message again". Verified live: Cancel kept the clock running; Ok paused; ticked the box, resumed, pressed Pause again, no notice; it holds for that sitting only.

Your requirements were concrete, so I built them rather than blocking; they're logged in the capture as Q15. If you want the Codex review pass on PR #121, say so.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
